A8.22c. Foreign activities reporting to the Of fice of Foreign Activities: 

(a) Fishery Mission to Mexico.-Headquar ters, Mexico, D. F. Pursuant to an Intergovernmental agreement, the Mission aids the Directorate of Fisheries of Mexico in the development and execution of fishery research, development and conservation programs. and in the development and execution of its activities and programs. (b) Fishery Mission to Peru.-Headquarters,

Lima, Peru. The Mission in cooperation with the Food Supply Division of The Institute of 

Inter-American Affairs furnishes technical assistance to the Peruvian Government in the management and conservation of the fishery resources of the country. The Chief of the Mission acts as advisor to the Government of Peru in the establishment and staffing of an organization for the conservation of fishery and wildlife resources 

(c) Philippine Fishery Program.—Headquar ters, Manila, Republic of the Philippines. Pursuant to the Philippine Rehabilitation Act, the Service maintains a fishery research laboratory, an experimental processing plant, and three research vessels which are in a diversified program of biological, oceanographic, and technological research; exploratory and experimental fishing; and economics designed to rehabilitate, modernize and develop the fishing industry and to aid in the conservation of the fishery resources. The Philippine 

Bureau of Fisheries, under an intergovernmental agreement, is cooperating in the project. 

A8.22d. Field activities of the Office of Pacific 

Oceanic Fishery Investigations, headquarters at

Honolulu, Hawaii, conducting research and development in south Pacific waters fisheries. 

A8.22e. Field stations of the Office of River 

Basin Studies located within the Missouri River Basin conduct surveys of water development projects within that basin and make basin-wide studies of fish and wildlife resources. Station headquarters is at Billings, Montana; there are three sub-stations.

A8.23. Alaska Game Commission.-(a) The 

Alaska Game Commission located in Juneau is a statutory advisory body created to promote the conservation of game and fur animals, birds, and game fishes. Regulations relating to these wildlife resources are recommended by the Commission to the Secretary for adoption, and, in addition, the Commission may prescribe certain special regulations. 

(b) The Alaska Game Commission is composed of four resident citizens of Alaska appointed by the Secretary of the Interior and the executive officer of the Commission, a representative of the 

Fish and Wildlife Service. The executive officer is designated by the Director of the Service and is responsible for the administrative aspects of the 

Commission's functions.

ESTABLISHMENT 

Creation. The Geological Survey was established in the Department of the Interior, in 1879, by statute.

Purpose. The Geological Survey is primarily a fact-finding agency which collects, distributes, and makes available, information about the mineral and water resources of the Nation. The Geological Survey conducts research in geology and related fields, prepares and distributes topographic maps, and supervises the technical mineral operations on leased public, Indian, and 

A9.01. Office of the Director.-The Director and the Assistant Director formulate bureau policy and exercise general control over the activities of the Geological Survey and its programs of scientific and engineering work. 

A9.02. Office of the Executive Officer.-The 

Office of the Executive Officer consists of four branches which provide administrative facilities for the Geological Survey, including map reproduction and distribution.

A9.02a. The Accounts Branch maintains fiscal records and audits the field accounts.

A9.02b. The Personnel Branch administers the personnel program. 

A9.02c. The Service and Supply Branch pro vides mails, files and messenger service, and purchases equipment and supplies. 

William E. Wrather.

A9.02d. The Map Reproduction Branch pre pares and prints geologic, topographic and other maps and charts. certain acquired lands, and on the naval petroleum reserves.

HEADQUARTERS ORGANIZATION General description.-The Geological Survey has a headquarters and four divisions in Washington, D. C. The field offices report to the four 

Divisions in charge of the respective activity. 

The Director as the chief executive of the Survey has charge of all of its activities. The Office of the Executive Officer is responsible for administrative services.

A9.03. Conservation Division.-The Conserva tion Division, through four functional branches, each with a Chief of Branch and supporting organization headquartered at Washington, D. C., examines and classifies Federal lands in the United States, territories, and possessions, as to their mineral character and water-power and water-storage values. The Division, in cooperation with the Bureau of Land Management, also supervises technical operations under oil and gas and other mineral leases on Federal, Indian, and naval petroleum reserve lands, and maintains accounts of the production and royalty accruals therefrom. The Division maintains for the Secretary of the Interior and the Bureaus under his direction, a consulting service on technical problems of public-land administration.

A9.03a. The Mineral Classification Branch per forms activities designed to prevent alienation of Government-owned mineral lands under inappli- 55

Organization Handbook cable laws, and to effect the conformity of land acquisitions pursuant to the mineral leasing laws with the terms and provisions of the various acts involved. The duties include the determination of Federal-land areas to be withdrawn from entry for purposes of mineral investigation and classification, the conduct, directly or through the Geologic Division, of the geologic surveys prerequisite to such classification, the preparation and promulgation of the classification of the lands involved as mineral or nonmineral, and the initiation of subsequent orders of restoration of release of lands from withdrawal. Under the mineral leasing laws the Branch determines and defines the "known geologic structure" of producing gas and oil fields as the basis for discrimination between the lands subject to lease by application or by competitive bidding at public auction; determines, for each oil and gas lease application that the land sought is, or is not, within the known geologic structure of a producing field, appraises and reports of the Bureau of Land Management first discoveries of oil and gas fields or deposits pursuant to applicable law for their pertinent effect on rental and royalty liabilities; and conducts comparable classification activities involving lands valuable for coal, oil, shale, phosphate, potash, sodium, etc., under the mineral leasing laws. 

A9.03b. The Mining Branch supervises operations and activities concerned with the prospecting, development, and production of various minerals, fuels, and fertilizers under leases on Federal and Indian lands subject to the various mineral leasing laws. The duties include advance approval of operating plans and procedures, or necessary deviation therefrom; field inspections for the measurement of and the acocunting for the minerals produced to assure a proper determination of royalty liabilities; periodic and frequent visits to effect the enforcement of regulations governing mining and milling methods and the safety and welfare of employees; field investigations and studies looking to the improvement of recovery and milling practices; the estimation of the reserves of coal and other minerals in Federal lands; the preparation of engineering reports and recommendations as to the leasing matters for the guidance of appropriate administrative officials in the Interior Department.

A9.03c. The Water and Power Branch classified the public lands as to their water-power and waterstorage possibilities. The duties and responsibilities of the Water and Power Branch require field investigations necessary for the collection of basic data on the water resources of the public lands, including topographic, geologic, and geophysical surveys; office investigations incident to the preparation of related maps, charts, and reports; the segregation in permanent reserves of lands that will be needed eventually for purposes of power development or water storage; the elimination from such reserves of lands found to be valueless for power or water-storage purposes; the processing of applications for rights of way on or across public lands for power structures, conduits, canals, and transmission lines; the recommendation of appropriate action to be taken by the Federal Power Commission on petitions for favorable determinations under section 25 of the Federal Power Act; the supervision of construction and operations pursuant to power permits or grants by the Secretary of the Interior and the collection of rentals thereunder; the preparation of reports to the Bureau of Land Management on the water-resource value of lands sought under a variety of publicland laws; and the furnishing to State and Federal agencies and to individuals and corporations, concerned with the conservation and development of stream-flow capacities, of pertinent data bearing on the problems of water power, irrigation. flood control, navigation, and water supplies for domestic and industrial use in the public-land states.

A9.03d. The Oil and Gas Leasing Branch super vises operations for the prospecting, development. and production of oil and gas from leases on Federal, Indian, and certain naval petroleum reserve lands. The duties of the branch include the advance approval of development plans and operating procedures; the periodic inspection of leased properties to assure orderly and efficient development of oil and gas deposits through proper engineering control and laboratory assistance, and the maintenance of proper records of production: the determination of the royalty liability of each producer; the maintenance of a consultative engineering service for the benefit of operating companies that are unable to maintain engineering staffs of their own; the estimation of petroleum reserves in public-land holdings; the enforcement of Federal regulations to assure orderly development, best engineering practices, prevention waste.

Geological Survey 57 and safety and welfare of workmen; the accumulation and correlation of stratigraphic, geologic, production and graphic records for use in engineering studies; the study and development of improved production techniques applicable to any given field to assure optimum production rates and to prevent underground waste of oil and uneconomical expenditure of reservoir energies; the analysis of oils, gases, waters, cores, etc., in furtherance of engineering endeavor, and the preparation of maps and reports for public information describing the results of the foregoing investigations. 

A9.04. Geologic Division.

A9.04a. The Mineral Deposits Branch makes field and laboratory studies of metallic and nonmetallic minerals and continuing appraisal of the Nation's mineral resources for publication as maps and reports. 

A9.04b. The Fuels Branch makes field surveys of the areal, structural, stratigraphic and economic geology of areas productive or potentially productive of oil, gas, coal, and oil shale for publication as maps and reports. 

A9.04c. The Alaskan and Foreign Geology

Branch makes field and laboratory studies to discover and evaluate the mineral resources of Alaska, and under the auspices of the State Department, makes studies of the mineral resources of foreign countries, principally Latin America. 

A9.04d. The General Geology Branch makes surveys of areas whose potential resources are not known, of geology of soils, and of geomorphic, placial, structural, and volcanic geology of regions for publication as maps and reports. 

A9.04e. The Engineering Geology Branch makes field surveys and laboratory studies of construction raw materials and of construction sites for highways, dams, tunnels and other structures for publication as maps and reports. 

A9.04f. The Military Geology Branch prepares confidential terrain intelligence reports on foreign areas for the Army and Navy and makes field surveys of the geology of foreign areas occupied by the United States forces for administrative use and for publication as maps and reports. 

A9.04g. The Geophysics Branch makes exploratory geophysical surveys-magnetic, gravimetric, seismic, electrical and geothermal—and correlates and interprets the results with structural and stratigraphic geology for publication as maps and reports. 

A9.04h. The Geochemistry and Petrology

Branch makes analyses and identification of minerals, rocks, and ores, and determines their properties, characteristics, and classification for use of Survey and other Federal agencies and for publication as reports and maps. 

A9.04i. The Paleontology and Stratigraphy 

Branch makes field and laboratory studies of vertebrate, invertebrate, and plant fossils for the correlation and age determination of the rock units for use of other geologists and for publication as maps and reports. 

A9.05. Topographic Divisi o n.-The Topo graphic Division is charged with the execution of field surveys and the preparation of maps, charts, and bulletins delineating the physical features of land areas in the United States, its territories, and its island possessions. Operating functions are conducted through 4 area field offices. 

A9.05a. The Plans and Coordination Branch is a staff facility which establishes survey programs and coordinates the work of the field offices. 

A9.05b. The Research and Technical Control 

Branch conducts studies of engineering techniques and establishes technical standards for the conduct of field operations.

A9.06. Water Resources Division.-The Water 

Resources Division investigates the quantity, quality, and availability of surface and underground waters in the United States, territories, and possessions. Its results appear as water-supply reports of the Geological Survey and papers or reports by cooperating States. The Division has two staff branches and three operating branches. 

A9.06a. The Technical Coordination Branch furnishes technical advice to the Chief of the Division and provides technical service to Division personnel for the achievement and maintenance of high technical standards and use of improved techniques in all Division investigations and in reports thereof. It coordinates interrelated technical activities of the operating branches. 

A9.06b. The Program Control Branch advises and assists the Chief of Division in the negotiation, control, and expedition of work programs and in the proper allotment and use of funds therefor. 

It maintains liaison among Division and Survey

Organization Handbook field offices and with cooperating parties as desirable for the efficient and successful initiation and prosecution of work. 

A9.06c. The Surface Water Branch measures the flow of streams and collects, compiles, and analyzes other basic data relating to surface-water resources, including utilization and flood and drought studies.

A9.06d. The Ground Water Branch investigates A9.20. General Description.-The Geological 

Survey is organized in the field along the lines of the functions performed by each of its four Divisions. Regional organization is related to the particular Divisions. The sections immediately following give the field organization of the Divisions. The locations of the field establishments are shown in the following table. 

FIELD ORGANIZATION A9.21. Conservation Division.-The field of fice organization of the Conservation Division follows the same functional pattern as is followed at headquarters. The Mineral Classification 

Branch conducts its field activities through five regional geologists, each with a small staff of specialists. The field organization of the Mining 

Branch is composed of six regional offices, each with a small staff headed by a Mining Supervisor. 

The Water and Power Branch maintains one regional office and three district offices, reporting thereto. Each district office has a small staff of specialists headed by a District Engineer. The 

Oil and Gas Leasing Branch maintains five regional offices to which district offices headed by 

District Engineers report. Each regional office reports to its respective Branch Chief at headquarters.

A9.22. Geologic Division.-The field work of this Division is conducted largely by small groups of geologists assigned to specific projects, involving mineral investigations and regional geologic surveys throughout the United States, territories, and Island possessions. Reports of investigations and surveys are prepared by the individual geologists in the field and are examined and reviewed the waters that lie below the surface-their source, occurrence, quantity, and head; and their conservation and utilization by means of wells and springs.

A9.06e. The Quality of Water Branch collects and analyzes data concerning the quantity and character or organic and inorganic substances in solution and in suspension and the physical properties of surface and ground waters. 

A9.30. Inquiries and requests in general.—

Information concerning the policies and programs of the Geological Survey may be obtained by adby competent specialists assigned to Branch Offices.

As the majority of the projects require the cooperation of specialists in several fields of geology and related sciences, the work is organized functionally and its technical direction is by specialists on the staffs of nine branches, Mineral Deposits, Geology of Fuels, Alaskan and Foreign Geology, Geophysics, Geochemistry and Petrology, Paleontology and Stratigraphy, General Geology, Engineering Geology and Military Geology. Four regional offices and seven field offices assist in coordinating the field work and report directly to the Division. 

A9.23. Topographic Division.-There are four regional offices, each under the direction of a regional engineer, responsible directly to the Chief of the Division. There is one additional project office, a photogrammetric unit organized for cooperative work with the Tennessee Valley Authority. Each regional office carries on within its geographic area all operational functions of mapmaking, including field surveys and office compilation, as well as the administrative activities incident thereto in accordance with the policies, objectives, and standards established by the Chief of the Division and his staff. 

A9.24. Water Resources Division.-The entire work of this Division is supervised directly through the three operating Branches; Surface 

Water, Ground Water, and Quality of Water.

Field offices and laboratories under each Branch, totalling 125 locations, report directly to the Branch.

Purpose. The National Park Service promotes and regulates the use of national parks, monuments, and similar reservations in conformity with the statutory mandate "to conserve the scenery and the natural and historic objects and the wildlife therein and to provide for the enjoyment of the same in such manner and by such means as will 

HEADQUARTERS A10.01. General description.-The Director of the National Park Service is in charge of the Service. The headquarters organization consists of the Director, Associate Director, and Assistant 

Director, 12 divisions, and the Chief Clerk's Office. 

Under the supervision of the Director, the headquarters staff formulates policies for the protection, preservation, and use of the national-park areas. It directs the interpretative services in natural sciences, history, and archaeology rendered for the public, provides for museum develop-

General description.-The National Park 

Service is composed of a headquarters staff in 

Washington, D. C., four regional offices and 13 types of field activities, such as national parks, monuments, and historic sites. Each activity is under the supervision of a superintendent who reports to the regional director. Regional directors in turn report to the Director of the Service. 

ORGANIZATION 

ments, and investigates proposed additions to the national-park system.

The Development, Land and Recreational Planning, Concessions, and Safety Divisions, and 

National Capital Parks are under the general administrative supervision of the Associate Director.

The History, Natural History, Forestry, Fiscal, and Personnel Divisions and the Chief Clerk's Of fice are under the general administrative supervision of the Assistant Director. 64

The Information Division, the United States Travel Division, and the Legal Division, on general legal matters, are under the general super vision of the Director. The Legal Division is under the general supervision of the Associate Director or the Assistant Director on legal matters affecting the divisions and offices for which they are responsible.

A10.02. Concessions Division.-The Conces sions Division supervises and maintains a complete surveillance of commercial operations conducted by private enterprise under contract with the Department of the Interior to carry on concession business within National Park Service areas for the accommodation of persons visiting those areas. 

A10.02a. The Operations Branch studies opera tion, development, maintenance, and management of concession activities and recommends policies and procedures involving the type of service rendered and general activities of concessioners. 

A10.02b. The Contracts Branch conducts stud ies to determine need of new or expanded concession facilities. Solicits proposals upon expiration of concession contracts. Determines advisability of securing competitive bids for concessions. 

Investigates potential concessioners as to their financial and business ability and experience. Determines contract requirements and negotiates concession contracts. Initiates policies affecting contract matters.

A10.02c. The Rates Branch determines and recommends for approval rates to be charged for all types of services furnished by concessioners to the public in National Park Service areas. 

A10.02d. The Commercial Audit Branch super vises and reviews audits of concessioners' accounts. 

Makes special studies and reports of financial conditions and other accounting problems affecting concession operations.

A10.03. Development Division.-The Develop ment Division coordinates the planning, architectural and landscape design, and engineering work on roads, trails, parkways, buildings, and other physical improvements.

A10.03a. The Architectural Branch reviews architectural designs, plans, specifications, and estimates for buildings and structures. grading, planting, and preservation of the natural landscape. 

A10.03b. The Landscape Architectural Branch reviews landscape architectural designs, specifications, and master plans; the arrangement of facilities within developed areas; and plans for the 

A10.03c. The Development Program Branch coordinates project construction programs, master plans, project plans, estimates, and priority schedules for all development proposals. 

A10.03d. The Parkways Branch coordinates parkway development projects and review plans, specifications, estimates, right-of-way maps, and easement and access plans. 

A10.03e. The Major Roads Branch coordinates the major road work performed cooperatively by the National Park Service and the Public Roads Administration. 

A10.03f. The Engineering Design Branch pre pares and reviews engineering designs, cost estimates, and construction contracts. Coordinates investigation and study of engineering projects. 

A10.03g. The Electrical Branch prepares and reviews designs, specifications, and cost estimates for electric systems, power plants, transmission lines, and radio and telephone systems. 

A10.03h. The Water Resources Branch coordi nates activities relating to acquisition, perfection, and protection of needed water rights.

A10.031. The Project Branch prepares estimates and plans and coordinates execution of soil and moisture conservation programs. Reviews and makes recommendations relating to special use per mits relating to the Development Division. 

A10.03j. The Minor Roads Branch reviews plans and recommends location, types, and general design for minor roads and trails construction and maintenance. 

A10.04. Forestry Division.—The Forestry Di vision supervises fire-control work, and the preservation and protection of park and monument forests from injurious insects and diseases and from preventable damage. Coordinates grazing studies and range management. Cooperates with other bureaus and agencies in fire and insect and treedisease control, and supervises forest planting, wood preservation and utilization, and forest studies.

A10.04a. The Administration and Tree Preser vation Branch correlates the administrative functions of the Division. Coordinates tree preservation, forest insect and tree disease control, and camp ground protection.

A10.04b. The Fire Control Training Branch coordinates training of personnel in fire preven-

Organization Handbook tion and fire suppression methods, and fire control safety techniques. Conducts studies of fire suppression operations and techniques and renders special assistance to field officials in fire emergencies.

A10.05. History Division.-The History Divi sion coordinates historical and archeological activities relating to the areas in the National Park System. Develops plans for the acquisition, preservation, and use of historic and archeologic sites and buildings.

A10.05a. The Historical Investigations Branch conducts historical research relating to existing and proposed areas. 

A10.05b. The Preservation and Use Branch coordinates matters relating to development, preservation, and use of historic and archeologic areas. 

A10.05c. The Archeological Investigations

Branch conducts archeological research relating to existing and proposed areas. 

A10.06. Land and Recreational Planning Division. The Land and Recreational Planning 

Division coordinates the investigation and acquisition of lands in existing and proposed areas, and adjustments of boundaries. Handles proposals for naming geographical features. Supervise river basin studies and reservoir recreational area development and management programs. 

Cooperates with states and their political subdivisions in planning for their park, parkway, and recreational area program and facilities. 

A10.06a. The Real Estate Branch coordinates the Service's land acquisition program and maintains land acquisition status records. 

A10.06b. The Land Planning Branch coordi nates the Service's land planning program.

A10.06c. The Economics and Statistical Branch coordinates economic and statistical information on National Park System areas. Prepares maps, charts, and graphic material required for the work of the Division, and maps and illustrations for special publications and reports. 

A10.06d. The State Cooperation Branch coordi nates cooperation with the states and their political subdivisions and with semi-public agencies in planning for their park, parkway, and recreational area programs. Supervises the investigation of surplus Federal properties desired by the states and their political subdivisions for park and recreational purposes.

A10.06e. The River Basin Planning Branch co ordinates the investigation and planning of recreational resources of reservoirs and other watercontrol projects under the jurisdiction of the  and the Corps of Engineers.

A10.06f. The Reservoir Development and Man agement Branch coordinates development and management of Bureau of Reclamation reservoir recreation areas.

A10.07. Natural History Division.-The Natu ral History Division coordinates the scientific research and interpretation of natural history; planning, preparation, and installation of educational services, exhibits, and facilities; wildlife management; and matters pertaining to hiking, mountain climbing, camping, and winter use.

Serves as technical adviser to the Director on these matters. 

A10.07a. The Biology Branch coordinates pro gram of study, protection, and management of wildlife, including fish. Plans and supervises biological work. Prepares biological management and restoration plans. 

A10.07b. The Geology Branch gives technical advice on the protection and preservation of geological formations and features; water supply; foundations; roads and trails; and erosion control. 

Cooperates in preparing information on the geo logic story of  areas. 

A10.07c. The Museum Branch renders technical advice and develops plans required for protection of the historic and scientific collections in National Park Service museums and historic structures. Directs museum preparation laboratories and supervises installation of exhibits. 

A10.08. Legal Division.-The Legal Division supervises legal matters of the National Park 

Service. Renders legal advice concerning land acquisition; legislation affecting the National 

Park System; contracts and regulations of various uses of National Park Service areas. 

A10.09. Information Division.-The Informa tion Division supervises informational, editorial, and public relations work of the National Park 

Service. Is responsible for the preparation and issuance of all National Park Service publications. 

A10.10. United States Travel Division.-The United States Travel Division encourages travel in the United States.

Cooperates with private

travel and other agencies in collecting, publishing, and making available to them information with respect to publicly owned recreation areas and facilities, the economics of travel, and other matters for the purpose of encouraging travel. Undertakes the preparation of itineraries for distinguished foreign visitors.

A10.11. Chief Clerk's Office. The Chief 

Clerk's Office maintains general files, mailing lists, and filing system manual, and provides related office services. Maintains property accounting records and purchases office supplies and equipment for the Director's Office. 

A10.12. Fiscal Division.-The Fiscal Division supervises the fiscal work of the National Park 

A10.20. Regional Offices.-Each Regional Of fice consists of a Regional Director and a staff of regional professional and administrative employees. The regional director serves as the representative of the Director of the National Park Service concerning matters within the region, and is responsible for seeing that the policies of the Department of the Interior and the Service are understood and uniformly applied in the individual activities under his jurisdiction. The staff renders professional advice and assistance to the superintendents under the Regional Director's supervision. 

Typical regional office organization:

A10.20a. The Concessions Division conducts field examinations and reviews and analyzes concessioners' accounts, reports, and records for the data from which to determine rates and policies in exercising supervision and control of concessioners' activities.

FIELD ORGANIZATION 

A10.20b. The Office of the Chief Clerk coordinates all fiscal matters, property accounting, and personnel work. Receives and dispatches mail and maintains records and files. 

A10.20c. The Field Audit Division audits all fiscal, property, and other administrative records in the field offices and conducts special investigations.

Service, including appropriation estimates, allocation of funds, fund, cost and property accounting, audit of expenditures, and the procurement of supplies and equipment.

A10.20d. The Architectural Division prepares architectural plans, drawings, specifications, and estimates for physical improvements. Supervises architectural planning, designing, development, 846667-49- -6 

A10.13. Personnel Division.—The Personnel 

Division supervises and coordinates all personnel matters of the National Park Service in accordance with applicable legislation, civil-service rules and regulations, and the policies and procedures of the Department of the Interior.

A10.14. Safety Division.-The Safety Division plans and coordinates activities relating to the safety and health of employees of the Government and concessioners and of the public and the safety and protection of Government property. and construction, including design of concessioner facilities. 

A10.20e. The Landscape Architectural Division prepares landscape architectural plans, drawings, specifications, and estimates for physical improvements, roads, trails, and parkways. Supervises landscape architectural planning, designing, development, and construction. Cooperates with concessioners in preparation of their landscape plans.

A10.20f. The Engineering Division supervises engineering work (except major road construction), sanitation control, and water rights development work. Directs and maintains records of acquisition, distribution, and disposal of automotive and heavy equipment.

A10.20g. The Forestry Division plans and con ducts activities relating to the protection of forests and other vegetation from fire, injurious insects, tree diseases, and from preventable damage. Coordinates grazing studies and range management. 

A10.20h. The Land and Recreational Planning 

Division coordinates regional land-acquisition program. Supervises investigation and planning of recreational resources of water-control projects under the jurisdiction of other Federal agencies outside of the Missouri and Columbia river basins.

Cooperates with other Federal, State, and local agencies in planning for their park, parkway, and recreational area programs and facilities. Supervises investigation of surplus Federal properties

Organization Handbook desired by the states and their political subdivisions for recreational purposes.

A10.201. The History Division coordinates all historical activities relating to the selection, development, preservation, and interpretation of areas. Conducts historical research. 

A10.20j. The Archeology Division coordinates all archeological activities relating to the selection, development, preservation, and interpretation of areas. Conducts archeological research. 

A10.20k. The Natural History Division studies and coordinates scientific and interpretive work pertaining to geological, biological, and other natural features. Directs museums and curatorial activities. Promotes library and visual aids facilities. Serves as a technical adviser to Regional Director.

A10.201. The Legal Division serves as legal ad viser to the Regional Director. Prepares and reviews contracts, permits, and regulations. Interprets and applies pertinent laws, court decisions, and established Departmental regulations and policies.

A10.20m. The River Basin Recreation Survey 

Division because of the magnitude and scope of the work involved in the Missouri and Columbia river basins has separate division respectively, in Regions Two and Four to conduct investigations and evaluate recreational resources of water-control projects of the  and the Corps of Engineers and to plan for their protection, development, and use. 

All of the above divisions are not represented in each regional office. For example, there is no 

Natural History, Legal, or River Basin Recreation 

Survey Divisions in the Region One Office; no 

Archeology Division in the Region Two Office; no 

History, Legal, or River Basin Recreation Survey 

Divisions in the Region Three Office;

and no Ar cheology Division in the Region Four Office. 

A10.21. Field Activities.-There are thirteen classifications of field activities. The work of a field activity logically falls into administrative, protection, maintenance and construction, and research and interpretive divisions. A superintendent is in direct charge of his activity and supervises all operations. He is responsible for maintaining the policies and rules and regulations of the . 

Typical national park area organization:

A10.22. Office of the Superintendent.-Super vises all activities within the park, including the concessioners furnishing facilities, services and supplies to the public under contract with the Department of the Interior. Interprets and applies all phases of National Park Service policies in the administration of the park and has general supervision over all planning and development therein.

A10.22a. The Protection Division supervises protection and preservation of entire park area; protection of forests and wildlife; traffic control and law enforcement; sale of entrance permits; special investigations; safety; and the furnishing of general park and directional information. 

A10.22b. The Office of the Chief Clerk supervises all work relating to accounting, budget estimates, procurement, warehousing and mess operations, property accounting; per diem wage scales, general files, and duplicating work. 

A10.22c. The Naturalist Division plans and conducts interpretive program of lectures and guided trips. Conducts research on biological and geological features pointing toward their interpretation and protection. Operates museums and other educational facilities. Prepares popular and scientific literature on features. 

A10.22d. The Engineering Division supervises all maintenance and construction activities involving engineering elements.

A10.22e. The Communications and Electrical 

Division supervises operation and maintenance of telephone and radio communication lines and equipment, hydroelectric and Diesel electric plants and lines, and building electrical installations. 

A10.22f. The Sanitation Division operates and maintains water and sewage systems together with appurtenant lines and equipment; plumbing and heating installations; garbage and refuse disposal; and sanitation systems. 

A10.22g. The Mechanical and Shop Division operates garage and repair shops for maintenance and repair of all motorized and mechanical equipment, including Diesel electric power plants. 

All of the national park areas are organized and operated in the same general manner. Differences in internal organization, where they exist, are due almost entirely to variations in the size of the organization. For example, a small national monument may have only a superintendent, ranger, and maintenanceman who would perform the administrative, protection, and maintenance

operations with the aid of technical assistance from the regional office.

A10.23. Office of National Capital Parks.— The Office of National Capital Parks is a field office of the National Park Service and is located in Washington, D. C. This Office is charged with administrative jurisdiction over the National

Capital Parks in the District of Columbia and its environs embracing 750 reservations, including the

George Washington Memorial Parkway and 

Prince William Forest Park in Virginia, and the

Catoctin Recreational Demonstration Area and PLACES TO OBTAIN INFORMATION AND MAKE REQUESTS 

A10.30. Inquiries and requests in general.—

General information concerning the functions, policies, programs, and activities of the National 

Park Service, or concerning the National Park 

System as a whole, may be obtained by addressing 

The Director, National Park Service, Department of the Interior, Washington 25, D. C.

Inquiries concerning a specific park, monument, or other area within the National Park System should be addressed to the superintendent in charge, who can also supply free informational literature. Inquiries concerning areas under the jurisdiction of the Office of the National Capital 

Parks should be addressed to the Superintendent, National Capital Parks, National Park Service, 

Department of the Interior, Washington 25, D. C. 

Requests for reservations for overnight accommodations in national park, monument, or other National Park Service areas, should be addressed to the authorized concessioner within such area, if known; otherwise, the request may be addressed to the superintendent in charge of the area. Applications for permits with respect to activities on, or use of, National Park Service areas should be submitted to the superintendent in charge of the area, subject to certain exceptions.

The National Park Service publishes free information publications relating to most of the areas in the National Park System. In addition, the Chesapeake and Ohio Canal in Maryland.

This Office also has administrative jurisdiction over the Lee Mansion National Memorial in Vir ginia, the Battleground National Cemetery, the 

House Where Lincoln Died, the Lincoln Memorial, the Lincoln Museum, the Thomas Jefferson Memorial, and the Washington Monument in Washing ton, D. C. The Office of the National Capital 

Parks is under the immediate supervision of a 

Superintendent, and the general supervision of the Associate Director of the National Park Service. 

there are a number of publications for sale, dealing with historical areas, the geology, fauna and flora of the parks, etc., which may be obtained from the Superintendent of Documents, Government Printing Office, Washington, D. C., from whom lists of such publications may also be obtained. Lists of publications, both free and for sale, are also obtainable from the Director, Na tional Park Service. 

A10.31. Location of headquarters organization. The Office of the Director of the National 

Park Service and the offices of the chiefs of the divisions are located in Washington, D. C.

ESTABLISHMENT Creation. The Bonneville Power Administra tion was established by statute in 1937. It is an agency of the Department of the Interior.

Purpose. The Bonneville Power Administra tion is directed by statute to encourage the widest possible use of electric energy and to provide markets therefor by constructing, operating, maintaining, and improving such power stations and transmission lines as may be necessary. Power generated at the Bonneville and Grand Coulee projects is distributed throughout Oregon and Washington.

The Administration now wholesales power to the extent of approximately 60 percent of the entire electric power supply of the Pacific Northwest. 

The Administration also has been designated power-marketing agency for new Federal dams to be constructed in the Columbia River Basin. 

General description.-The Bonneville Power 

Administration, headed by the Administrator, is composed of a headquarter's staff located at Portland, Oregon, and five district offices located at points in the Pacific Northwest. In addition there are several advisory boards and committees established to consult with and advise the Administrator on matters of program and policy, and to coordinate major programs of the Bonneville

Power Administration with programs of other Government agencies engaged in related activities throughout the Pacific Northwest. 

Advisory boards and committees.-(a) The 

Bonneville Act provided that the Administrator "shall act in consultation with an advisory board composed of a representative designated by the 

Secretary of War, a representative designated by the Secretary of the Interior, a representative designated by the Federal Power Commission, and a representative designated by the Secretary of Agriculture."

Since March 1946, with the establishment of the 

Columbia Basin Inter-Agency Committee, the 

Bonneville Advisory Board has been composed of regional representatives of the above agencies. 

The Bonneville Power Administrator has taken up

Bonneville Advisory Board matters at the regular monthly meetings of the Columbia Basin Inter- 

Agency Committee, the membership of which includes the members of the Bonneville Advisory Board. 

(b) The Columbia Basin Inter-Agency Com mittee was established by the Federal Inter- Agency River Basin Committee in 1946, to facilitate cooperation in the investigation and preparation of reports on multiple-purpose water projects and the correlation of the results of these investi gations. The membership of the Columbia Basin 

Inter-Agency Committee is identical with the membership of the Bonneville Advisory Board and also includes a representative of the Department of Commerce. (c) The Regional Advisory Council informs the 

Bonneville Power Administrator on problems, policies, plans and programs of the region that are related to or affected by the Administration's power policies. Composed of representative lead ers from agriculture, labor, industrial, financial, business, educational and other regional interests 73

Organization Handbook and groups who serve voluntarily without pay by invitation of the Administrator and Council, this body enables the Administrator to check at more or less frequent intervals with a cross section of regional opinion and to secure the benefit of a wide

A11.04. Division of Information.-The Divi sion of Information assembles general information with respect to the market area or region and its problems and needs and develops and carries out informational programs designed to acquaint the general public and public groups with the objectives and activities of the Administration.

A11.07b. The Branch of Procurement and 

Stores procures all materials, equipment, supplies, and services (other than personal); prepares and issues invitations to bid, reviews bids and awards construction contracts; negotiates rentals and sales of equipment and materials; receives, stores, issues or assigns and controls all materials, supplies, and services; salvages returned materials.

A11.07c. The Branch of Land and Office Service acquires, disposes and manages land and interests in land, and obtains and grants licenses and other rights and privileges pertaining to land and structures; negotiates with other Federal agencies for the acquisition of surplus real property and related equipment; manages buildings and grounds, both owned or leased (other than operating electric plant and specific facilities otherwise assigned); controls, maintains passenger automotive and airplane transportation services; furnishes duplicating and photographic services. 

A11.08. Division of Power Management.- The Division of Power Management develops, formulates and executes policies and activities relating to the marketing and sale of power; determines the extent and location of future power loads within the region required for planning new generation and transmission capacity to meet such needs; analyzes power production capabilities of present and proposed generating plants of the sysitem to ascertain amounts of power which will be [ available for sale on the system under various future conditions and for various combinations of plants; studies economic feasibility of proposed 

5 additions and changes in the system; collaborates 

Bonneville Power Administration in preparation of the construction budget; prepares the yearly Advance Program Report outlining facilities needed in the forthcoming sixyear period to meet power requirements of the region; develops wholesale power rate structures; reviews distributors' resale rates and their financial and operating status under these rates; maintains power statistics; determines essential contract elements and provisions for all power contracts; coordinates power contract negotiations; prepares power bills. 75 system additions, interconnections, plan of service, and construction programs.

A11.09. Division of Engineering.-The Divi sion of Engineering formulates, develops, and executes policies and activities of the Administration relating to the engineering, design, construction, operation and maintenance of the Administration's power facilities; conducts engineering studies, tests, and experiments to improve reliability of operations and economic functioning of the Administration's system; makes engineering studies, including recommendations, pertaining to 

A11.09a. The Branch of System Engineering conducts system engineering and cost studies and prepares engineering recommendations therefrom for system additions, customers' facilities, system acquisitions and interconnections, and the longrange construction program; prepares engineering justifications for short-range program, makes engineering studies, tests and basic research experiments in regard to the power, relay, protection, and communication systems and equipment, and prepares recommendations therefrom to develop simplification of design and improvement in the operation and functioning of the Administration's system.

A11.09b. The Branch of Design and Inspection designs transmission lines and substations and related power-system facilities; establishes, coordinates, and supervises construction schedules; prepares specifications relating to engineering materials and equipment and contract construction work; supervises, inspects, and expedites performance of contractors in the manufacture and delivery of materials and equipment and of construction work in the field; exercises custody of engineering materials in the field and during process of construction; provides design, drafting, and architectural services.

A11.09c. The Branch of Operations operates all power facilities of the Administration; dispatches and schedules all power supplied by and to the Administration; schedules storage and release of water controlled by the Administration; administers contractual operation requirements; makes short-range load and resource studies; coordinates the operation of the Administration's system with other interconnected systems; operates and maintains metering, relaying, communication, and laboratory facilities, and performs related activities. 

A11.09d. The Branch of Maintenance patrols and maintains all transmission lines, substations, and associated power facilities; controls, maintains, and assigns tools, vehicles, and work equipment; operates and maintains the Administration's garage and shop facilities; performs all force account construction work.

Chief, Division of Engineering.- ESTABLISHMENT Creation. The Southwestern Power Adminis tration was created by the Secretary of the Interior in 1943 to market power from the Denison Dam 

Project and the Norfolk Dam Project. Subse quently the Administration was charged with the responsibility of marketing power produced by projects of the Corps of Engineers, United States Department of the Army, in the Southwestern part of the United States.

Purpose. The Administration's objectives are to market hydroelectric power at the lowest possible rates to consumers to encourage the widest possible use of its power and in so doing to provide market outlets by construction, interconnecting, operating, and maintaining such transmission lines and substations as are necessary. 

The area of operation is the States of Arkansas and Louisiana, that part of Kansas and Missouri south of the Missouri River Basin and east of the 98th Meridian, and that part of Texas and Oklahoma lying east of the 99th Meridian and north of the 

San Antonio River Basin.

A12.01. Administrator. — The Administrator of the Southwestern Power Administration is the chief executive. He reports through the Division of Power to the Secretary of the Interior. The 

Office of the Administrator formulates policies, plans, and procedures for carrying out the objectives of the Administration, and directs its operation.

A12.02. Staff offices.-The staff offices consist of: A12.02 The Division of Law for legal matters, including litigation, contracts and land acquisition; A12.03 The Division of Finance for budget, finance, accounting and auditing functions;

A12.04 The Division of Administration and Per sonnel for property, office services, and personnel functions.

General description.-The Southwestern Power Administration consists of a Headquarters 

Office at Tulsa, Oklahoma; offices at Little Rock, Arkansas; Washington, D. C.; and a Texas-Lou isiana Area Office at Denison, Texas. The Head quarters Office at Tulsa, Oklahoma, is made up of the Administrator with staff and technical divisions.

HEADQUARTERS ORGANIZATION A12.10. Division of Operations.-The Division of Operations plans and directs the dispatching and transmission of power through the facilities of the Southwestern Power Administration and associate organizations; maintains transmission lines, substations and other facilities; performs all meter, relay, testing and communications functions.

A12.11. Division of Engineering. The Divi sion of Engineering plans and performs design and engineering studies and surveys for transmission lines, substations and other facilities; supervises construction; and renders technical advice on operation and maintenance. It also supervises and directs sales activities and the development of markets and resources for power sales. 79

Organization Handbook A12.12. Division of Land.-The Division of 

Land performs all work necessary for acquisition of lands, maintains records and maps of land

A12.21. Area offices.-The Texas-Louisiana 

Area Office is responsible for the activities of the Administration within the States of Louisiana under jurisdiction of the Southwestern Power Administration, and handles all claims arising from land acquisition.

A12.30. Inquiries and requests.-Information concerning the policies and programs of the Administration, wholesale power rates, power contracts, procedure, availability of supply and construction plans may be obtained by addressing the

A13.00. THE ALASKA RAILROAD General Manager---- 

J. P. Johnson.

John E. Manley. 

Acting Assistant General Manager_ Special Representative of the General Manager (Seattle, Wash.)---- A. R. Sessions. 

Creation.-The Alaska Railroad was estab lished as an agency within the Department of the Interior in 1914 to stimulate settlement and the industrial and agricultural development of the Territory by providing transportation within the Territory, and by developing areas along the lines of the Railroad.

General description.-The Alaska Railroad, headed by the General Manager, is composed of the headquarters staff located at Anchorage,

Alaska, and the Office of the Special Representative of the General Manager, located at Seattle, Washington.

A13.01. Organization.-The Office of the Gen eral Manager includes the General Manager, the 

Assistant General Manager (Operations), the Assistant to the General Manager (Finance and 

Administration), the Assistant to the General 

Manager (Traffic and Development), and twenty departments under the direction of these officials. 

A13.02. The General Manager is in general charge of all activities of the Railroad which includes the operation of a railroad line totalling almost 500 miles running from Seward and Whittier to Fairbanks in the interior, and the management of tourist hotels at Curry and in Mount McKinley Park. He is responsible to the Secretary of the Interior, reporting through the Director of the Division of Territories and Island Possessions. 

A13.03. The Assistant General Manager (Operations) acts for the General Manager in his absence. He has major responsibility for all operations activities, except rehabilitation activicies, and the following operating departments report directly to him:

A13.03a. The Stores and Purchases Depart ment conducts the activities relating to the procurement and accounting for all materials, supplies, equipment and other property, prior to ssuance to another department of the Railroad. 

A13.03b. The Communications Department conducts activities relating to the installation, maintenance, operation, and improvement of all communication equipment. 

A13.03c. The Operations Department conducts activities relating to the operation of all rolling stock, receiving, storing, hauling, and issuance of all freight, transportation of passengers, operation of ocean docks, river lines and river docks, and other operations assigned to the Railroad. 

A13.03d. The Engineering Department conducts all activities relating to the construction and maintenance of roadway and track, bridges and buildings, and all other physical facilities. 

A13.03e. The Motive Power and Equipment 

Department conducts all activities relating to operating condition and maintenance of all rolling stock, automotive equipment, miscellaneous work equipment and floating equipment, and is responsible for the operation of all motive power and equipment shops, including power houses. 

A13.03f. The Safety Department plans, establishes, and promulgates safety, fire prevention and protection measures, and is responsible for the operation of the Anchorage Fire Department.

A13.03g. The Special Agents Department has under its jurisdiction protection of all railroad property and all railroad policing work. 

A13.04. The Assistant to the General Manager (Finance and Administration) is responsible for fiscal, budget, and organizational policy and has major responsibility for all activities of the following departments which report directly to him:

A13.04a. The Personnel and Labor Relations 

Department conducts, plans and coordinates all phases of personnel work and labor relations activities. 

A13.04b. The Medical Department is in charge of medical and surgical services, physical examinations, Anchorage Clinic, health and sanitation, and 81

Organization Handbook has supervision of contract surgeons and hospitals.

A13.04c. The Accounts and Audits Department conducts all auditing, accounting and disbursing activities and prepares operation statistics and financial reports.

A13.04d. The Budget and Finance Department prepares studies, reports, and recommendations concerning appropriations, statistics and general financial matters. It also prepares the annual budget.

A13.04e. The Hotels, Commissary, and Housing 

Department conducts all activities relating to commissary, hotels, and mess-hall operations, as well as all matters pertaining to the housing of The 

Alaska Railroad's employees. 

A13.05. The Assistant to the General Manager (Traffic and Development) is responsible for the activities of the following departments: 

A13.05a. The Claims Department reviews, processes and determines amount to be paid on loss and damage freight and baggage claims and supervises the sale or disposition of damaged and unclaimed consignments.

A13.05b. The Rates and Traffic Department studies, recommends, compiles and distributes rates and promotes freight traffic.

A13.05c. The Advertising and Publicity De partment prepares editorial and publicity programs, including all photographic activities, and the preparation of advertising.

A13.05d. The Industrial Development Depart ment formulates, promulgates and supervises plans and policies for promotion of agricultural and industrial enterprises adjacent to the Railroad. 

A13.05e. The Tourists and Excursions Department formulates plans and conducts all activities relating to the promotion of passenger traffic, managing tours and making excursion arrangements. 

A13.06. The following departments report directly to the General Manager:

A13.06a. Legal Department. This depart ment which is under the jurisdiction of the Counsel reviews and advises the General Manager and the department heads on all legal matters. 

A13.06b. Rehabilitation Department. This department consists of a committee composed of the Chief Engineer as Chairman, who acts as liaison officer in coordinating rehabilitation work of the department concerned, Superintendent of Motive Power and Equipment, and the Superintendent of Operations. This

This group is responsible for the planning, programming, initiating, and all subsequent work necessary to complete the physical rehabilitation program of the Railroad. 

A13.06c. Real Estate and Contract Department. This department is under the jurisdiction of the Real Estate and Contract Agent, and conducts all activities relating to agreements for the lease, rental, or sale of any real property, equipment, or utilities owned by the Railroad. 

A13.20. Office of the Special Representative of the General Manager, Seattle, Washing ton. The Special Representative of the General 

Manager at Seattle represents the General Manager in the handling of the Railroad's relationship with rail and steamship lines in the States and the general public. He is also responsible for personnel recruitment, acquiring surplus property, and expediting purchases and contracts.

A14.00. ALASKA ROAD COMMISSION Commissioner of Roads for Alaska………. 

Chief Engineer_. 

Creation. The Alaska Road Commission was established in the Department of the Interior in 1932 to administer the functions vested in the Secretary of the Interior relating to the construction and maintenance of roads, trails, and appurtenant works in Alaska.

Purpose. The Alaska Road Commission is re sponsible for the location, design, construction, repair and maintenance of roads, bridges, ferries, trails, and other works in the Territory of Alaska, except areas within national forest reserves. 

General description.-The Alaska Road Com mission, headed by the Commissioner of Roads for Alaska, is composed of a headquarters staff located at Juneau, Alaska, and four district offices located at Anchorage, Fairbanks, Valdez, and 

Nome, and a subdistrict office at Haines, Alaska.

A14.01. Headquarters organization. The functions and programs of the Alaska Road Commission are under the general direction of the 

Commissioner of Roads for Alaska. The Commis sioner is responsible for the formulation of policies and programs and for the over-all direction of the work. He is responsible to the Secretary of the Interior, reporting through the Director of the Division of Territories and Island Possessions. 

He is assisted by the Chief Engineer, who is responsible under his general supervision for the conduct of operations and represents him in his absence. The headquarters staff consists of the 

Office of the Commissioner of Roads and four main divisions. 

The Commissioner of Roads for Alaska may ex ercise the authority conferred upon the Secretary of the Interior with respect to the functions of che Alaska Road Commission.

A14.02. Administrative Division.-The Ad ministrative Division is responsible for the conduct of all phases of administrative management, including budget, finance, personnel, administrative reports, office services, and supply and property 

John R. Noyes. 

Ike P. Taylor. management. It exercises staff supervision over the conduct of such administrative work as is performed in the several district offices. 

A14.03. Engineering Division.—The Engineer ing Division is responsible for the conduct of engineering investigations and the preparation of engineering reports, for the preparation and review of designs, plans, and specifications, and for the collection, evaluation, and utilization of engineering cost data. It exercises staff supervision over the conduct of district office surveys. 

A14.04. Contracts Division.-The Contracts 

Division is responsible for supervision of all engineering construction by contract. In conjunction with the Engineering Division, it prepares the engineering features of such contracts, reviews contracts prepared by the Public Roads Adminis tration, architectural and engineering firms or others, and prepares alterations, additions, supplemental agreements and change orders. It also handles contract inspection and priorities, expediting, etc.

A14.05. Construction Division. - The Con struction Division has general responsibility for all engineering construction and maintenance by force account, utilizing the district organization of the Alaska Road Commission for actual operations. It exercises supervision over road and bridge construction, maintenance and operation of road-building equipment, and supply, as well as mess operations. It advises and makes recommendations to the Administrative Division on the types of road-building equipment to be purchased. 

It is also responsible for supervision of maintenance by force account of roads, bridges, and other structures constructed or administered by the Alaska Road Commission. 

A14.20. Field Organization.-The headquar ters organization of the Alaska Road Commission, in addition to formulating policies and programs, and establishing technical guides concerning the 83

Organization Handbook construction of roads in Alaska, has immediate supervision over the work of the district offices and one subdistrict office.

A14.21. Districts.-District offices are maintained in Valdez, Anchorage, Fairbanks, and

Nome, Alaska. A subdistrict office is maintained in Haines, Alaska. These offices cover the entire 

Territory of Alaska, except areas within national forest reserves. The District Superintendents are responsible to the Commissioner of Roads for Alaska for the development and execution of road and related administrative work in their respective districts in accordance with predetermined policies and programs and under the technical guidance of the headquarters staff. Their work includes supervision of survey parties, contract inspectors, forceaccount supervisors, repair shops, accounts, messes, and all other activities connected with the construction of roads in their respective districts.

A15.00. THE VIRGIN ISLANDS CORPORATION President_._. 

Creation.-The Virgin Islands Corporation is a Government Corporation created by Public Law 

149, Eighty-first Congress, approved June 30, 1949, in order to promote the general welfare of the inhabitants of the Virgin Islands of the United States through the economic development of the islands. The Corporation succeeds The Virgin 

Islands Company which was established by an ordinance of the Colonial Council of St. Thomas and St. John, Virgin Islands, in 1934.

General description.-The management of the 

Corporation is vested in a Board of Directors consisting of seven members including the Secretary of the Interior, the Secretary of Agriculture, the 

Chairman of the Reconstruction Finance Corpo ration, the Governor of the Virgin Islands, and three experienced businessmen appointed by the 

President of the United States. The Chairman of the Board is selected by the Board of Directors.

The officers of the Virgin Islands Corporation are appointed by the Board of Directors. The principal officer is the President of the Corporation who is responsible for carrying out the programs and policies of the Board and for the day-to-day activities of the Corporation.

Purpose. The Corporation operates and main tains sugarcane lands, a sugar factory; a hotel; a mail, cargo and passenger schooner; an abattoir; and a chicken farm. The hotel (Bluebeard's Castle 

Norman S. Olson.

Hotel on the Island of St. Thomas), the abattoir, and the chicken farm are at the present time leased to private operators. Other activities include the rental of tractors and other equipment to farmers in the area and the operation of a rock crusher. 

The Corporation is authorized by law to engage in activities such as tourist development, soil and water conservation, lending to farmers and small industries, agricultural diversification, transportation, and other programs designed to develop the basic economy of the islands.

A15.02. Sugar Division. The Corporation cultivates approximately 3,000 acres of sugarcane land, maintains livestock and agricultural equipment, and operates the Island's only sugar factory. 

This factory is the only outlet for all of the sugarcane grown on St. Croix. The Sugar Division also advises and assists independent farmers in connection with their agricultural problems. 

A15.03. Accounting Division.-This division conducts all financial and accounting operations of the Corporation.

A15.04. Rural Electric Division.-The Rural 

Electric Division maintains and operates a rural electrification plant on the Island of St. Croix and sells power directly to rural consumers on the Island. Excess power is sold to the St. Croix 

Power Authority for consumption in Christiansted and Frederiksted. 85

A16.00. PUERTO RICO RECONSTRUCTION ADMINISTRATION James P. Davis. 

Guillermo Esteves.

Administrator__ 

Assistant Administrator___. 

Creation and purpose.-The Puerto Rico Re construction Administration was established as an agency within the Department of the Interior in 1936 to administer projects for providing relief and for increasing employment in Puerto Rico, with emphasis on rehabilitation of the Island's agricultural economy. The projects consist principally of the management and maintenance of housing projects, the construction of additional rural housing units in distressed areas of the Island, the granting and servicing of loans to cooperatives and the operation of a demonstration farm for assistance to needy resettlers.

General description.-The Puerto Rico Recon struction Administration, headed by the Administrator, is composed of the headquarters staff, located at San Juan, Puerto Rico, and the Administrator's Office, which serves as the liaison office in Washington, D. C. The functions of the Administration relating to the  and the Washington Office of the Administration are handled through the Division of Territories and Island Possessions. The San Juan headquarters staff is composed of the Office of the Assistant 

Administrator, Office of Agricultural Liquidation, Office of Housing Management, and four divisions, viz., Engineering, Legal, Finance, and Miscellaneous.

A16.01. Office of the Assistant Administra tor. The Assistant Administrator conducts the administration of the Program as delegated by the Administrator.

A16.02. Office of Agricultural Liquidation.- 

This office has the responsibility for planning, directing and affecting an orderly liquidation of certain agricultural programs. 

A16.03. Office of Housing Management.- 

This office manages and operates the rural and urban housing projects. 

A16.04. Engineering Division. This division conducts all activities relating to the construction and maintenance of rural and urban houses and service buildings; repair of roads and streets, etc. 

A16.05. Legal Division. This division which is under the jurisdiction of the Assistant General 

Counsel reviews and advises the Administrator and Assistant Administrator on all legal matters. 

A16.06. Finance Division.-This division con ducts all activities pertaining to finance, auditing, and accounting; has general supervision over the 

Office of Housing Management; purchasing contracts; financial reports and statistics. 

A16.07. Miscellaneous Division. Included within the scope of this division is work connected with the following activities of the Administration: Loans to Cooperatives, Surveying Unit,

Transportation and Garage, Disposition of Remaining Lands at Lafayette, Central Mail Services, Building Maintenance and Custodial 

Services. 

Inquiries and requests in general concerning the Territories.-Information concerning the 

Division of Territories and Island Possessions or any of the territorial activities under its jurisdiction may be procured by addressing the Director, Division of Territories and Island Possessions, Department of the Interior, Washington, D. C., or by writing directly to the territorial office. 87
